Write: elements/willow/sheets.py
"""Stage 6: the technique as one parameterised call, swept on labelled sheets.

willow_cell(size, density, season, hour, sun_az, sun_el, camera, distance, seed) -> RGB cell.
  size     : bush width in pixels (3..150); the level of detail follows it (willow.lod_params)
  density  : 0 sparse, open, see-through .. 1 dense, closed
  season   : summer | spring | silvery | autumn
  hour     : morning | noon | golden | dusk | overcast (sun colour, ambient, key)
  sun_az/el: sun direction (az 0 = from the left, 90 = from behind the viewer, 180 = right, 270 = behind)
  camera   : 'low' (eye near the ground: the stem hollow shows) | 'oblique' (30 deg from above:
             the crown shows more top, the base hides, the cast shadow lies on the gravel)
  distance : 0 near .. 0.8 far (depth palette: darks lift and cool, lights stay)
"""
import numpy as np, os, sys
from wpaint import *
from willow import paint_bush, BAND, LEAFS
from palette import willow_palette
from context import gravel, scene_palette
from stage4 import sky_ground

OUT = "sheets"; os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)


def rotate_x(L, deg):
    a = np.radians(deg)
    x, y, z = L
    return np.array([x, y * np.cos(a) - z * np.sin(a), y * np.sin(a) + z * np.cos(a)])


def density_params(d):
    """Sparse willows (young, browsed, flood-thinned) are open: fewer wands, smaller clumps, more
    stems and sky showing; dense ones are closed domes."""
    return dict(form=dict(nw=int(14 + 26 * d), lobe_r=(0.05 + 0.03 * d, 0.09 + 0.04 * d), lobe_gap=1.2 - 0.5 * d,
                          skirt_n=int(1 + 3 * d), crown_w=0.3),
                stems=dict(see=1.0 + 0.6 * (1 - d), zone_t=0.22 - 0.1 * (1 - d)))


def willow_cell(size=90, density=0.7, season="summer", hour="noon", sun_az=25, sun_el=40, camera="low",
                distance=0.0, seed=1, cw=None, ch=None):
    cw = cw or int(max(48, size * 1.35 + 20)); ch = ch or int(max(40, size * 0.95 + 24))
    gy = ch - 8 if camera == "low" else ch - 12
    horizon = gy - 6 if camera == "low" else int(ch * 0.18)
    cv = sky_ground(cw, ch, horizon)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    gm = np.zeros((ch, cw), bool); gm[horizon:] = True
    gravel(cv, gm, rng, sun(sun_az, sun_el), y_near=ch, y_far=horizon, stone=(0.8, 2.5 if camera == "low" else 3.0), dens=0.35)
    L = sun(sun_az, sun_el)
    Hf = 0.72
    p = density_params(density)
    if camera == "oblique":
        L = rotate_x(L, -30)       # seeing the bush from 30 deg above = tilting the light the other way
        Hf = 0.62
        p = dict(p); p["stems"] = dict(p["stems"], see=0.0)
        # cast shadow on the gravel, away from the sun
        L0 = sun(sun_az, sun_el)
        lxz = np.hypot(L0[0], L0[2]) + 1e-6
        off = size * 0.45 / max(np.tan(np.radians(sun_el)), 0.3)
        sx = cw / 2 - L0[0] / lxz * off * 0.8
        sy = gy - size * 0.1 + L0[2] / lxz * off * 0.35
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:ch, 0:cw]
        sh = (((xx - sx) / (size * 0.52)) ** 2 + ((yy - sy) / (size * 0.22)) ** 2 < 1) & gm
        cv.step[sh & (cv.mat == GRAVEL)] = np.minimum(cv.step[sh & (cv.mat == GRAVEL)], 1)
    band = int(np.clip(round(distance / 0.27), 0, 3))
    paint_bush(cv, cw / 2, gy, size, size * Hf, L, rng, band=band, p=p if size >= 7 else None)
    depths = (0.0, 0.3, 0.55, 0.8)
    pal = scene_palette(season, hour, depths)
    return cv.render(pal)


def pad_to(img, w, h, bg=(24, 24, 28)):
    out = np.zeros((h, w, 3), np.uint8); out[:] = bg
    y0 = (h - img.shape[0]) // 2; x0 = (w - img.shape[1]) // 2
    out[y0:y0 + img.shape[0], x0:x0 + img.shape[1]] = img
    return out


def sheet(rows, cols, fn, rowlab, collab, title, path, k=2):
    cells = [upscale(fn(r, c), k) for r in rows for c in cols]
    H = max(c.shape[0] for c in cells); W = max(c.shape[1] for c in cells)
    cells = [pad_to(c, W, H) for c in cells]
    save(label_grid(cells, len(cols), rowlabels=rowlab, collabels=collab, title=title), path)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    which = sys.argv[1:] or ["season_hour", "size_distance", "density_seed", "camera_light", "silver_season"]
    if "season_hour" in which:
        S = ["summer", "spring", "silvery", "autumn"]; Hh = ["morning", "noon", "golden", "overcast", "dusk"]
        sheet(S, Hh, lambda s, h: willow_cell(90, 0.7, s, h, seed=2), S, Hh,
              "sheet A: season x hour (size 90, density 0.7, sun az 25 el 40, low camera)", f"{OUT}/A_season_x_hour.png")
    if "size_distance" in which:
        sizes = [140, 90, 56, 34, 20, 11, 6, 3]; dists = [0.0, 0.3, 0.55, 0.8]
        sheet(dists, sizes, lambda d, s: willow_cell(s, 0.7, "summer", "noon", distance=d, seed=3, cw=190, ch=140), [f"distance {d}" for d in dists],
              [f"{s} px" for s in sizes], "sheet B: size (level of detail) x distance (depth palette); summer noon", f"{OUT}/B_size_x_distance.png", k=1)
    if "density_seed" in which:
        dens = [0.0, 0.35, 0.7, 1.0]; seeds = [11, 12, 13, 14, 15]
        sheet(dens, seeds, lambda d, s: willow_cell(90, d, "summer", "noon", seed=s), [f"density {d}" for d in dens],
              [f"seed {s}" for s in seeds], "sheet C: density x seed (size 90, summer noon)", f"{OUT}/C_density_x_seed.png")
    if "camera_light" in which:
        cams = ["low", "oblique"]; suns = [(20, 35), (60, 50), (160, 35), (200, 25), (265, 25)]
        sheet(cams, suns, lambda c, sn: willow_cell(90, 0.7, "summer", "golden", sn[0], sn[1], camera=c, seed=4),
              [f"camera {c}" for c in cams], [f"sun az {a} el {e}" for a, e in suns],
              "sheet D: camera x sun direction (size 90, summer golden)", f"{OUT}/D_camera_x_light.png")
    if "silver_season" in which:
        S = ["summer", "spring", "silvery", "autumn"]; sizes = [120, 60, 30]
        sheet(sizes, S, lambda z, s: willow_cell(z, 0.8, s, "noon", camera="oblique", seed=6, cw=170, ch=130), [f"{z} px, oblique" for z in sizes], S,
              "sheet E: season x size, oblique camera, noon", f"{OUT}/E_season_x_size_oblique.png")

    ground_contact(cv, new, gy, W_, rng, foot, band)

def ground_contact(cv, new, gy, W_, rng, foot=None, band=0):
    \"\"\"How a bush sits: the darkest line where it meets the ground, a dark undercut pocket in the
    ground just under its middle, and stones or tufts overlapping its foot.\"\"\"
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    cols = np.nonzero(new.any(0))[0]
    if not len(cols):
        return
    xa, xb = cols.min(), cols.max()
    for x in cols:
        yb = np.nonzero(new[:, x])[0].max()
        rel = abs(x - (xa + xb) / 2) / max((xb - xa) / 2, 1)
        for k in (1, 2):
            y = yb + k
            if y < h and cv.mat[y, x] in (GRAVEL, GRASS):
                if k == 1 or rel < 0.55:
                    cv.step[y, x] = 0 if (k == 1 and rel < 0.8) else min(cv.step[y, x], 1)
    if foot and W_ >= 14:
        n = int(W_ / 6)
        for _ in range(n):
            x = int(rng.uniform(xa + 2, xb - 2))
            col = np.nonzero(new[:, x])[0]
            if not len(col):
                continue
            yb = col.max()
            if foot == "gravel":
                a = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
                for dx in range(-a, a + 1):
                    xx = x + dx
                    if 0 <= xx < w and 0 <= yb < h:
                        cv.mat[yb, xx] = GRAVEL; cv.step[yb, xx] = 1
                        if yb - 1 >= 0 and abs(dx) < a:
                            cv.mat[yb - 1, xx] = GRAVEL; cv.step[yb - 1, xx] = 4 if dx <= 0 else 3
            elif foot == "grass":
                for dx in range(int(rng.integers(1, 4))):
                    L_ = int(rng.integers(2, 5)); xx = x + dx * 2
                    for k in range(L_):
                        y = yb + 1 - k
                        if 0 <= xx < w and 0 <= y < h:
                            cv.mat[y, xx] = GRASS; cv.step[y, xx] = 4 if k >= L_ - 1 else 2""")
# micro: always at least a dab
w=w.replace("""    if not m.any():
        m[int(gy) - 1, int(cx)] = True""","""    if m.sum() < 3:
        # a willow of 3 px is still a dab: two dark pixels at the foot, one lit on top
        c, g = int(round(cx)), int(gy)
        for (xx, yy_) in ((c, g - 1), (c + 1, g - 1), (c, g - 2)):
            if 0 <= xx < w and 0 <= yy_ < h:
                m[yy_, xx] = True""")
w=w.replace("""        if y == int(gy) - 1:
            mat, st = LEAFS, 0""","""        if y == int(gy) - 1:
            mat, st = LEAFS, 0
        if m.sum() <= 4 and y == np.nonzero(m)[0].min():
            mat, st = LEAF, 4""")

Bash: Cast shadows everywhere, reflection compression, varied rows
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow && python3 - <<'E'
c=open('context.py').read()
c+='''

def cast_shadow(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, fore=0.18, rng=None):
    """A bush's shadow on the ground: the ground itself in its shadow family (two steps down), the
    stones keep their marks. An ellipse from the foot toward the side away from the sun, longer as
    the sun drops; foreshortened by the camera (fore: low camera ~0.15, 30 deg oblique ~0.35)."""
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    el = np.degrees(np.arcsin(np.clip(L[1], -1, 1)))
    lxz = np.hypot(L[0], L[2]) + 1e-6
    reach = H_ * 0.55 / max(np.tan(np.radians(max(el, 8))), 0.25)
    dx = -L[0] / lxz * reach * 0.8
    dy = -L[2] / lxz * reach * fore * 2.0
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w].astype(float)
    ax = W_ * 0.5 + abs(dx) * 0.45; ay = max(1.5, W_ * fore * 0.5 + abs(dy) * 0.45)
    ecx, ecy = cx + dx * 0.5, gy - 1 + dy * 0.5
    d = ((xx - ecx) / ax) ** 2 + ((yy - ecy) / ay) ** 2
    edge = 1.0 if rng is None else 1.0 + 0.25 * (rng.random((h, w)) - 0.5)
    sh = (d < edge) & (yy >= gy - 1 - max(0, -dy)) & np.isin(cv.mat, (GRAVEL, GRASS))
    cv.step[sh] = np.maximum(cv.step[sh].astype(int) - 2, 0)
    return sh
'''

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow && cat >> LESSONS.md <<'E'

---

## Stage 2: stems and leaves

![near willow](stage2/s2_near.png)

A near willow, 150 px wide. What made it a willow rather than a generic shrub:

- **The mark is a spray, not a speck** (`foliage.willow_spray`). A 1-px twig along the local wand
  direction (a clean run, 3–8 px), with narrow leaves 2–3 px long, 1 px wide, leaving it every 1–2
  px. The leaves **hang**: 85% go to the side of the twig that points them downward, turned 30–55°
  off the twig and pulled down, so neighbouring leaves run near-parallel. My first sprays
  alternated sides and made X's and asterisks (critic, round 5: "lace or snowflakes").
- **Leaves gather in the light and simplify in shadow** (`stage2/s2_light_shadow_base.png`). Sprays
  are laid on the clump field from stage 0: dense and at the top step on clump caps, one or two steps
  dimmer below, none in the clump's dark band. In the shadow family the base is the near-dark ramp
  with sparse dim marks. The shadow side needs *some* marks: with none it is the black polygon again.
- **Stems are narrow forms, so clean runs.** Wands are drawn as polylines through every fourth wand
  point, each segment snapped to 1:1, 2:1, 3:1 steps. Drawn through every second point they zigzag
  and read as cracks (round 5). At the base, 55% of the wands show, broken where foliage crosses
  them, mostly one step above the dark hollow; only stems leaning to the sun and in front get the
  lit step. My first stems were all visible and all lit: a barcode, or a picket fence.
- **Wands between clumps**: 40–60% of the wands show as broken arcs between clumps, only where the
  pixel is dark base. This is the willow's arching structure; it is also the easiest thing to overdo.
- **A few sprays hang in front of the stem hollow**, so the base is never a clean fence.
- **The crest**: lit wand tips (1–5 px runs past the silhouette with leaflets) on the sun side,
  dark ticks on the shadow side, 1–2 px holes and caps (`fringe`), and airy holes in the upper crown
  where a clump's dark band meets the silhouette.

![light, shadow, base](stage2/s2_light_shadow_base.png)
![compare](stage2/s2_compare.png)

**Honest comparison.** Against the Salix triandra photo the leaf scale and the hanging are right. At
near scale a Ferrari foreground bush has much more value contrast (near-black base, saturated lights):
that is a palette-by-depth matter, fixed in stage 3 by darker near bases. Not matched: his marks are
chunkier because his plants are broadleaf; mine are finer, which the photos support (the critic
switched to judging leaf form against the photos and craft against Ferrari after David's steer).

---

## Stage 3: colour

![palettes](stage3/s3_palettes.png)

One index canvas, eight palettes (`palette.py`). Swatches top-left of each cell: lit, silver, shadow,
twig, sky, gravel.

- **Ramps walk in OKLab from a cool dark to a warm light.** The dark end is pulled toward the sky's
  ambient colour, the light end toward the sun's colour, more so at golden hour and dusk.
- **Silver is its own ramp and its own material** (`SILVER`): the pale undersides of willow leaves,
  grey-green to near-white, cooler than the green. In a spray, 35% of the leaves on the underside of
  the twig are marked silver. In the "silvery" season the whole lit ramp shifts grey-green.
- **Autumn gold**: lit ramp ochre to gold, base olive-brown, silver pale straw, twigs orange-brown
  (I had them too bright; they read as orange columns, so the light twig step came down).
- **Hour sets a key**: dusk scales the foliage's lightness to 0.62, golden 0.88; the sky ramp and
  the sun/ambient pair change with it.
- **Near bases are dark.** The first palettes used his middle-distance hedge values for the near
  bush's base and it looked slate-grey and flat; near foliage needs a near-black base (his C1).
- **Depth** (`willow_palette(depth=)`) lifts the darks toward the haze strongly and the lights barely.

![silver](stage3/s3_compare_silver.png)
![autumn](stage3/s3_compare_autumn.png)

---

## Stage 4: clumps and bands, close to far

![LOD](stage4/s4_lod_3x.png)

- **Level of detail follows pixel width** (`willow.lod_params`): above 110 px, sprays; 30–110 px,
  the hedge speckle with its marks turned along the wands (hanging diagonals); 7–30 px, dots and pairs
  on a few clumps, no stems or tips; under 7 px, `paint_micro`, a lumpy blob with lit pixels on its
  sun-side top and a dark foot. At 3 px it is still a dab: two dark pixels and a lit one on top (it
  used to vanish; critic round 9). Marks never shrink below a pixel; a smaller bush gets fewer,
  relatively larger marks.
- **Clumps nestle by value** (`stage4/s4_clump_3x.png`): bushes are painted far to near; each new
  bush darkens the foliage just above its crest (two rows) into the shadow family, so a lit crest
  always sits against a dark valley.
- **Each bush is its own**: splay, droop, lean, crown width and clump count are drawn per bush.
- **The skirt droops to the gravel.** My wands made a shelf that jutted over bare ground (round 6);
  now the most splayed wands droop to near the ground and any column whose foliage stops above the
  ground gets a drooping curtain down to it, except over the stem hollow.
- **Bands** (`stage4/s4_band_2x.png`): rows receding in four depth bands (their own materials,
  `BAND` offsets, so one palette holds all depths).
- Every bush is painted in its own small canvas and placed (a 50-bush band went from >10 min to ~1).

![clump](stage4/s4_clump_3x.png)
![band](stage4/s4_band_2x.png)

---

## Stage 5: in context

`context.py` holds small painters for the setting, following the studio's measured rules.

![water](stage5/s5_water_edge_autumn_golden_3x.png)
![gravel bar](stage5/s5_gravel_bar_summer_noon_3x.png)
![grass](stage5/s5_grass_edge_summer_noon_3x.png)

- **Water**: an exact mirror in reflected materials (+150), lights dropped about 16 OKLab points,
  darks kept, chroma cut 20% and 28% toward the water colour; sky reflects a step darker near the
  viewer; a sheen band under reflected objects; horizontal dash rows; dark shallows at a shore.
- **Gravel**: rounded stones, larger near, pale/grey/dark ones mixed, a light top row and a dark
  underside only on real stones, a one-pixel cast shadow away from the sun. It is not my element and
  it still lays in courses (round 7); the channel student has the better gravel.
- **Contact** (`willow.ground_contact`, `context.cast_shadow`): the darkest line at the foot, a dark
  undercut pocket in the ground under the middle, stones or grass tufts overlapping the foot, and a
  cast shadow that is the ground itself two steps down (the stones keep their marks), longer as the
  sun drops, foreshortened by the camera. My first shadows were flat grey ellipses and read as holes
  (round 9).
- **Grass edge**: blades overlap the feet of the bushes, which is what finally sat them in the world.

---

## Stage 6: sheets

`sheets.py`: `willow_cell(size, density, season, hour, sun_az, sun_el, camera, distance, seed)`.

![A](sheets/A_season_x_hour.png)
![B](sheets/B_size_x_distance.png)
![C](sheets/C_density_x_seed.png)
![D](sheets/D_camera_x_light.png)
![E](sheets/E_season_x_size_oblique.png)

- A: season × hour. B: size (LOD) × distance (depth palette). C: density × seed. D: camera × sun
  direction. E: season × size, oblique camera.
- **Oblique camera**: seeing the bush from 30° above is the same as tilting the light the other
  way; the bush is flatter, the stem hollow hides, the ground contact is an ellipse (its front edge
  curves down, `base_arc`), and the cast shadow lies on the gravel.
- **Density**: sparse willows have fewer wands, smaller clumps, more stems and sky.

---

## Where it stands, honestly

- **Stage 0.** The copies are in his language but a skilled eye would tell them apart at 8×: C1's
  darks are flat black between clumps where his carry dim leaves; C2's clumps are too even in size.
- **The willow** reads as willow in colour at 60–150 px: hanging narrow leaves, arching wands, the stem
  fan, silver undersides, gold in autumn. The water-edge scene is the strongest image.
- **Still open** (from the critic's last rounds and my own look): the shadow side's contour is still
  smoother than his found edges; the far rows still repeat a bump shape; the oblique back-lit bush is a
  dark blob with a thin rim (back-lit willows should glow silver); gravel lays in courses; greyscale
  reads dimmer than colour.
- **What would help**: a reference set of willow on braided-river gravel photographed from 20–40° above
  (for the oblique camera), and a back-lit willow set. More species would interest me later: dwarf
  willow mats and alder scrub would reuse the whole pipeline with a new mark and a new wand model.
E
echo ok

Bash: Curved skirt hem instead of vertical wall
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow && python3 - <<'E'
s=open('bush.py').read()
old="""                if self.gy - bot > 2:
                    curtain[bot + 1:int(self.gy), xc] = True"""
new="""                # the hem rises toward the sides, so the skirt's outline curves down to the ground
                # instead of dropping as a vertical wall
                ax = abs(xc - self.cx)
                hem = self.H * 0.4 * np.clip((ax - 0.28 * self.W) / (0.22 * self.W), 0, 1) ** 1.3
                ybot = int(self.gy - hem)
                if ybot - bot > 1:
                    curtain[bot + 1:ybot, xc] = True"""
assert old in s
s=s.replace(old,new)
open('bush.py','w').write(s)

SubagentHandback:

The willow scrub study has all six stages drafted, plus a stage 0 where I copied Ferrari first. It is not at his level yet: at 8× a skilled eye can still tell my work from his crops.

Everything is in ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow/. LESSONS.md has each stage with its images, what I learned and an honest comparison. The critic has done 10 rounds in CRITIQUE.md; every verdict was REVISE and no stage has a PASS. The later rounds were mostly specific notes, and I acted on most of them.

Strongest results: the autumn golden-hour scene at the water's edge (stage5/s5_water_edge_autumn_golden_3x.png) and the season × hour sheet (sheets/A_season_x_hour.png). The critic singled out the palette system on that sheet as ready.

What makes it willow:

  • Stage 0 (the copy): I copied two leafy passages from Ferrari's Deep Forest. The technique I found is a heap of small clumps, each with a lit cap and a dark gap under it. Lit marks gather on the clump tops, and the edge of the mass is where the clumps end.
  • Form: about 30 arching stems fan up from the root and droop at the tips, with a dark hollow and visible stems at the base.
  • Leaves: a twig carries narrow 1-px leaves that hang near-parallel. They are dense in the light and sparse in shadow, and about a third of the undersides show silver.
  • Colour: one image is recoloured for four seasons (summer, spring, silvery, autumn gold) and five times of day. Lights warm toward the sun and darks cool toward the sky.
  • Close to far: detail simplifies with size, from full leaf sprays down to a 3-pixel dab that no longer vanishes.
  • Settings: a gravel bar, water with a toned-down reflection, and a grass edge. Every bush now casts a textured shadow and sits in the ground instead of on a flat line.
  • Sheets: five labelled sweeps from one function in sheets.py, covering size, density, season, hour, sun direction, camera and distance.

Still open:

  • Seen from above at 120 px, the bush turns into a haystack or pyramid shape (sheet E, top row).
  • The shadow-side outline is smoother than his broken edges.
  • The distant treeline repeats one bump shape.
  • A back-lit bush seen from above reads as a dark blob; a back-lit willow should glow silver.
  • The gravel lies in horizontal rows.
  • The greyscale versions read dimmer than the colour ones.

Requests (also in LESSONS.md): photos of willow on braided-river gravel taken from 20–40° above, and a set of back-lit willows. Dwarf willow mats and alder scrub would interest me later, since they reuse the same pipeline with a new leaf mark and branch model.

Bash: Backlit glow, dark twigs, rougher shadow edge
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow && python3 - <<'E'
s=open('bush.py').read()
old=s[s.index("            rl = mask & (rim > 0.22) & ~interior"):s.index("        x[~mask] = np.nan\n        lit = lit & ~interior")]
new='''            rl = mask & (rim > 0.22) & ~interior
            x = np.where(rl, np.maximum(x, 3.0 + 2.2 * np.clip(rim, 0, 1)), x)
            lit = lit | rl
            # transmitted light: willow leaves are thin, so with the sun behind the whole crown glows
            # wherever it is thin: strongest near the silhouette and in the upper crown, fading into
            # the thick core and the base. (Photo: backlit willow = pale glowing mass, dark twigs.)
            back = np.clip((-L[2] - 0.15) / 0.6, 0, 1) * self.glow
            if back > 0:
                lam = max(3.0, self.W * 0.13)
                upper = np.clip((self.gy - yy) / self.H, 0, 1)
                t = np.exp(-(edge - 1) / lam) * (0.45 + 0.55 * upper) + 0.35 * upper ** 2
                t = t * (0.7 + 0.6 * rn) * back
                gl = mask & (t > 0.28) & ~interior
                x = np.where(gl, np.maximum(x, 3.0 + 2.4 * np.clip((t - 0.28) / 0.5, 0, 1)), x)
                lit = lit | gl
'''
s=s.replace(old,new)
s=s.replace("        self.rim, self.rim_w = rim, rim_w\n","        self.rim, self.rim_w = rim, rim_w\n        self.glow = glow\n")
s=s.replace("diverge=0.32, rim=1.4, rim_w=3.5):","diverge=0.32, rim=1.4, rim_w=3.5, glow=1.0, curtain=1):")
s=s.replace("        self.curtain = 1\n","        self.curtain = curtain\n")
s=s.replace("        self.rim, self.rim_w = 0.0, 3.0\n","        self.rim, self.rim_w = 0.0, 3.0\n        self.glow = 0.0\n")
open('bush.py','w').write(s)

w=open('willow.py').read()
# backlit: twigs silhouetted dark over the glow, more of them
w=w.replace("""                if S[py, px] <= 1:
                    litp = MAT[py, px] == LEAF
                    wandpx.append((px, py, 2 if litp else 1))""","""                if S[py, px] <= 1 or backlit:
                    litp = MAT[py, px] == LEAF
                    wandpx.append((px, py, 0 if backlit else (2 if litp else 1)))""")
w=w.replace("""    wv = p.get("wands_visible")
    if wv:""","""    wv = p.get("wands_visible")
    backlit = L[2] < -0.3
    if backlit and wv:
        wv = dict(wv, p=min(1.0, wv["p"] * 1.6), brk=wv["brk"] * 0.6)
    if wv:""")
# shadow-side edge rougher
w=w.replace("""                        hole_p=0.2, bump_p=0.25, rim_p=0.0)""","""                        hole_p=0.3, bump_p=0.35, bump_w=(1, 3), peak_p=0.35, rim_p=0.0)""")

# output:
# Riparian vegetation: the river's forest

*Plant ecology desk. Field guide 04. Written 2026-09-25. Written to be read alongside the
hydrology desk's work on the Skokomish. The plant-river coupling runs both ways: the river
makes the ground the riparian plants need, and the plants (roots, logs, jams) steer the river.*

## 1. The riparian cast (west side)

| plant | where on the floodplain | role |
|---|---|---|
| **willows** (Sitka *Salix sitchensis*, Pacific *S. lasiandra*, Hooker's *S. hookeriana* near the coast, Scouler's on drier banks) | the lowest, wettest, youngest surfaces: gravel bars, bank toes, side channels | first woody colonists; slow the water, trap sand; resprout from any broken piece; stems bend under floods rather than break |
| **black cottonwood** *Populus trichocarpa* | young bars and low terraces, on the gravel-bed rivers | the pioneer giant; seedlings need bare wet sediment at exactly the right time |
| **red alder** *Alnus rubra* | bars, banks, low terraces, and any disturbed ground; the most abundant riparian tree in western Washington | nitrogen fixer; dense even-aged bands along almost every stream |
| **bigleaf maple** | higher terraces and valley-side toe slopes; old alluvial fans | moss-laden in wet valleys; resprouts |
| **Sitka spruce, western redcedar, western hemlock** | older terraces (100+ years), establishing under the cottonwood and alder | the eventual canopy; big conifers that fall in become the key wood in the river |
| **Oregon ash** | backswamps and sloughs, South Sound and SW Washington (not on the Olympic Peninsula) | |
| shrubs: **salmonberry**, red-osier dogwood, twinberry, Pacific ninebark, red elderberry, Indian plum, snowberry, **devil's club** on wet seepy terraces, **Himalayan blackberry** (invasive) on disturbed banks | under and between the trees | salmonberry thickets can hold ground against tree seedlings for decades |
| herbs: **skunk cabbage** in muck, horsetails on silty bars, lady fern, sedges and rushes, piggyback plant, youth-on-age, stinging nettle on rich silt; **reed canarygrass** (invasive) in open wet ground | | reed canarygrass forms dense 1.5 to 2 m swards that stop tree establishment and clog side channels: a dominant plant of the Skokomish valley's pastures and sloughs |

East side: black cottonwood, several willows, water birch, quaking aspen, thinleaf alder,
red-osier dogwood, chokecherry, hackberry and Woods' rose along streams through steppe; in the
coulees these strips are the only trees for kilometres (guide 09).

## 2. How the floodplain forest is built: the cottonwood "recruitment box"

Black cottonwood and willows need four things together to start from seed (Mahoney & Rood
1998; Braatne et al. 1996; Rood et al. 2003):

1. **Bare, moist mineral surface** (sand, silt or fine gravel) with no competing plants: a
   bar freshly built or scoured by the spring flood.
2. **Timing:** seeds are released over a few weeks, **late May to early July** (earlier at low
   elevation)

Write: elements/willow/species.py
"""More riparian and alpine shrubs on the willow pipeline.

Each species is a change to the form (the wand model), the mark, and the palette; the clump
grammar, the value plan, the fringe, level of detail, contact and depth bands are all shared.

- winter willow : leafless. Only the wands and their twigs, 1-px clean runs, coloured red, orange,
                  yellow or grey; far off the twig mass becomes a mauve-grey haze.
- sitka alder   : Alnus viridis ssp. sinuata. A sprawling shrub of avalanche tracks and cold banks;
                  stems curve out low and sweep up (J-shaped), broad serrate leaves: the broadleaf
                  lozenge glyph from the C1 copy instead of the willow spray.
- dwarf willow  : Salix arctica / nivalis / cascadensis. A mat hugging rock and ground, 5-15 cm
                  tall, lobed edge, tiny round leaves as dots, pale catkins upright in summer.
- red alder     : Alnus rubra. A small tree in bands along streams: 1-3 pale grey trunks mottled
                  with white lichen, leaning, a narrow rounded crown of broadleaf clumps.
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
from wpaint import *
from bush import WillowBush, wand_dirs
from willow import paint_willow, merge, LEAFS, BAND, FOLIAGE, lod_params, ground_contact, paint_micro
from foliage import poisson, stroke_run, snap_dir, lozenge, value_noise

BARK = 13          # alder trunk ramp (6 steps)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ winter willow
def paint_winter(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Leafless willow: every wand drawn full length as a clean run, with short twigs off it that
    angle upward; stems leaning toward the sun take the lit step, the rest the mid step; a darker
    tangle at the base; the upper twig ends thin out. Returns the painted mask."""
    p = p or {}
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    b = WillowBush(cx, gy, W_, H_, rng, **merge(dict(nw=int(18 + W_ * 0.25), crown_w=0.3, curtain=0), p.get("form", {})))
    lx = L[0]
    M = np.zeros((h, w), bool)
    def put(x, y, s):
        if 0 <= x < w and 0 <= y < gy:
            if cv.mat[y, x] == STEM and cv.step[y, x] >= s:
                return
            cv.mat[y, x] = STEM; cv.step[y, x] = s; M[y, x] = True
    step_every = max(2, int(3 * 90 / max(W_, 30)))
    for wd in b.wands:
        pts = wd["pts"]; n = len(pts) - 1
        idx = list(range(0, n + 1, 3)) + ([n] if n % 3 else [])
        poly = [(int(round(pts[k][0])), int(round(pts[k][1]))) for k in idx]
        lean = pts[n][0] - pts[0][0]
        base_s = 3 if lean * (-lx) < 0 else 2         # toward the sun: lit
        pix = polyline_clean(poly)
        for i, (x, y) in enumerate(pix):
            t = i / max(len(pix) - 1, 1)
            s = 1 if t < 0.12 else base_s
            if t > 0.9 and rng.random() < 0.4:
                continue                                 # the tips thin out
            put(x, y, s)
        # twigs: short runs off the wand in its upper 60%, angled up and out
        for k in range(int(n * 0.4), n, max(1, step_every // 2)):
            if rng.random() > p.get("twig_p", 0.55):
                continue
            x0, y0 = pts[k][0], pts[k][1]
            tx, ty = pts[min(n, k + 1)][0] - pts[k][0], pts[min(n, k + 1)][1] - pts[k][1]
            a = np.arctan2(ty, tx) + (1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1) * np.radians(rng.uniform(25, 45))
            ang = snap_dir(np.cos(a), np.sin(a) - 0.3)
            Lt = int(rng.integers(2, 3 + int(W_ / 30)))
            for j, (x, y) in enumerate(stroke_run(int(round(x0)), int(round(y0)), ang, Lt)):
                put(x, y, base_s if j < Lt - 1 else max(2, base_s - 1))
    # the base: a darker tangle of the oldest stems
    for x in range(int(cx - W_ * 0.2), int(cx + W_ * 0.2)):
        for y in range(int(gy - H_ * 0.12), gy):
            if 0 <= x < w and M[y, x]:
                cv.step[y, x] = min(cv.step[y, x], 1)
    return M


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ broadleaf glyph (alders)
def broadleaf_marks(mask, light, rng, top, dens=(0.05, 1.0), dens_lo=0.25, lens=(2.5, 4.0), wmax=(0.9, 1.5),
                    spacing=2.2, base=(0.1, 0.8), angles=(26.6, 45, 63), S=None):
    """The C1 lozenge leaf: lit upper half, one step darker lower half, a dark tuck under it,
    painted dim-to-bright; density and brightness from the clump field."""
    h, w = mask.shape
    if S is None:
        S = -np.ones((h, w), int)
    n = value_noise((h, w), 1.5, rng)
    t = 0.55 * rng.random((h, w)) + 0.45 * (n - n.min()) / (np.ptp(n) + 1e-9)
    S[mask] = 0
    S[(t < base[0] + (base[1] - base[0]) * light) & mask] = 1
    G = []
    for (x, y) in poisson(h, w, spacing, rng, mask=mask):
        l = light[int(y), int(x)]
        lp = np.clip((l - dens_lo) / (1 - dens_lo), 0, 1)
        if rng.random() > dens[0] + (dens[1] - dens[0]) * lp:
            continue
        tp = int(np.clip(round(1.2 + lp * (top - 0.6) + rng.normal(0, 0.45)), 1, top))
        a = np.radians(rng.choice(angles)) * (1 if rng.random() < 0.5 else -1)
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            a = np.pi - a
        G.append((tp + rng.random(), x, y, rng.uniform(*lens), a, rng.uniform(*wmax), tp))
    G.sort()
    for (_, x, y, L_, a, wm, tp) in G:
        pts = lozenge(x, y, L_, a, wm)
        low = {}
        for (px, py), role in pts.items():
            if 0 <= px < w and 0 <= py < h and mask[py, px]:
                S[py, px] = tp if role == "T" else max(0, tp - 1)
                low[px] = max(low.get(px, -1), py)
        for px, py in low.items():
            if rng.random() < 0.5 and 0 <= py + 1 < h and mask[py + 1, px] and (px, py + 1) not in pts:
                S[py + 1, px] = min(S[py + 1, px], max(0, tp - 2))
    return S


def paint_sitka_alder(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Sitka alder: the willow form made sprawling (stems sweep out low then up), broad leaves."""
    q = merge(lod_params(W_), dict(
        form=dict(spread=85, droop=0.15, diverge=0.55, crown_w=0.45, lobe_r=(0.08, 0.14), lobe_gap=0.8, top_flat=0.4,
                  nw=int(16 + W_ * 0.15)),
        clump=dict(r=(max(2.0, W_ * 0.05), max(3.5, W_ * 0.09)), aspect=1.2),
        sprays=None, broadleaf=True, stems=dict(p=0.5, see=1.2), front_sprays=None))
    return paint_willow(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, merge(q, p or {}))


def paint_dwarf_willow(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Dwarf willow mat: very low, spreading, lobed; tiny round leaves as dots and pairs; pale
    upright catkins scattered on top in summer (catkin_p)."""
    q = merge(lod_params(max(W_ * 0.6, 8)), dict(
        form=dict(spread=89, droop=0.05, diverge=0.8, crown_w=0.8, nw=int(12 + W_ * 0.2), lobe_r=(0.05, 0.09),
                  lobe_gap=0.6, top_flat=1.0, skirt_n=4, curtain=0),
        clump=dict(r=(1.5, 3.0), aspect=1.6), sprays=None,
        lit=dict(dir_p=0.0, glyphs=(("dot", 5), ("h2", 3), ("v2", 1), ("cap", 1)), dens=(0.1, 0.9)),
        stems=dict(p=0.0, see=0.0), front_sprays=None, tips=None, wands_visible=None,
        fringe=dict(hole_p=0.1, bump_p=0.3, bump_w=(1, 3), peak_p=0.05, rim_p=0.9)))
    r = paint_willow(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, merge(q, p or {}))
    cp = (p or {}).get("catkin_p", 0.0)
    if cp:
        M = r["M"]
        tops = M & ~np.roll(M, 1, 0)
        for y, x in zip(*np.nonzero(tops)):
            if rng.random() < cp and y - 2 >= 0:
                cv.mat[y - 1, x] = SILVER; cv.step[y - 1, x] = 4
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    cv.mat[y - 2, x] = SILVER; cv.step[y - 2, x] = 3
    return r


def paint_red_alder(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Red alder: 1-3 leaning pale trunks with white lichen patches, a narrow rounded crown of
    broadleaf clumps held on the upper 55% of the height."""
    p = p or {}
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    nt = int(rng.choice([1, 1, 2, 2, 3]))
    crown_bot = gy - H_ * 0.42
    trunks = []
    for i in range(nt):
        bx = cx + rng.uniform(-0.12, 0.12) * W_
        lean = rng.uniform(-0.12, 0.12) + (i - (nt - 1) / 2) * 0.12
        tw = max(1, int(round(W_ * rng.uniform(0.05, 0.08))))
        trunks.append((bx, lean, tw))
    # crown: the willow form, upright, sitting on top of the trunks
    Hc = H_ * 0.62
    q = merge(lod_params(W_), dict(
        form=dict(spread=45, droop=0.25, diverge=0.2, crown_w=0.35, lobe_r=(0.1, 0.17), lobe_gap=0.75,
                  nw=int(14 + W_ * 0.15), curtain=0, skirt_n=1, egg_c=0.5),
        clump=dict(r=(max(2.0, W_ * 0.06), max(3.5, W_ * 0.11)), aspect=1.15),
        sprays=None, broadleaf=True, stems=dict(p=0.0, see=0.0), front_sprays=None, tips=dict(p=0.08, lens=(1, 2))))
    q = merge(q, p.get("crown", {}))
    tmp = Canvas(w, h)
    paint_willow(tmp, cx, int(crown_bot + Hc * 0.35), W_, Hc, L, rng, q)
    crown = tmp.mat != 0
    # trunks first (behind the crown's lower clumps), then crown
    lx = -np.sign(L[0]) if abs(L[0]) > 0.05 else 1
    for (bx, lean, tw) in trunks:
        top_y = int(crown_bot - Hc * 0.25)
        for y in range(top_y, gy):
            t = (gy - y) / max(gy - top_y, 1)
            xc = bx + lean * (gy - y)
            half = max(0.5, tw * (1 - 0.35 * t) / 2)
            for x in range(int(np.floor(xc - half)), int(np.ceil(xc + half)) + 1):
                if not (0 <= x < w):
                    continue
                u = (x - xc) / max(half, 0.5)
                if abs(u) > 1.05:
                    continue
                s = 4 if u * lx < -0.2 else (3 if abs(u) <= 0.3 else 1)   # lit side, body, shadow side
                cv.mat[y, x] = BARK; cv.step[y, x] = s
        # lichen: white patches, and dark lenticel dashes across the trunk
        for y in range(top_y, gy):
            if rng.random() < 0.18:
                xc = bx + lean * (gy - y); xx = int(round(xc + rng.uniform(-tw / 2, tw / 2)))
                if 0 <= xx < w and cv.mat[y, xx] == BARK:
                    cv.step[y, xx] = 5
            if rng.random() < 0.08 and tw >= 3:
                xc = bx + lean * (gy - y)
                for xx in range(int(xc - tw / 2) + 1, int(xc + tw / 2)):
                    if 0 <= xx < w and cv.mat[y, xx] == BARK:
                        cv.step[y, xx] = 0
    cv.mat[crown] = tmp.mat[crown]; cv.step[crown] = tmp.step[crown]
    return crown


SPECIES = {
    "willow": None,
    "winter_willow": paint_winter,
    "sitka_alder": paint_sitka_alder,
    "dwarf_willow": paint_dwarf_willow,
    "red_alder": paint_red_alder,
}


def paint_species(cv, species, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, band=0, p=None, foot=None):
    """Any species at any band: paint in a local canvas, then place with the band's materials."""
    from willow import paint_bush
    if species == "willow":
        return paint_bush(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, band=band, p=p, foot=foot)
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    pw, ph_ = int(W_ * 1.8 + 16), int(H_ * 1.6 + 12)
    ox, oy = int(round(cx - pw / 2)), int(round(gy - ph_ + 2))
    tl = Canvas(pw, ph_)
    lcx, lgy = cx - ox, int(gy) - oy
    if W_ < 6 and species != "winter_willow":
        paint_micro(tl, lcx, lgy, W_, H_, L, rng)
    else:
        SPECIES[species](tl, lcx, lgy, W_, H_, L, rng, p)
    new = np.zeros((h, w), bool)
    ys0, ys1 = max(0, oy), min(h, oy + ph_); xs0, xs1 = max(0, ox), min(w, ox + pw)
    if ys1 > ys0 and xs1 > xs0:
        sub = tl.mat[ys0 - oy:ys1 - oy, xs0 - ox:xs1 - ox]
        m = sub != 0
        new[ys0:ys1, xs0:xs1] = m
        tgt_m = cv.mat[ys0:ys1, xs0:xs1]; tgt_s = cv.step[ys0:ys1, xs0:xs1]
        off = np.where(sub == BARK, 0, BAND * band)
        tgt_m[m] = (sub + off)[m]; tgt_s[m] = tl.step[ys0 - oy:ys1 - oy, xs0 - ox:xs1 - ox][m]
    if species != "winter_willow":
        ground_contact(cv, new, gy, W_, rng, foot, band)
    return new

"""Stage 7: more species on the willow pipeline. A species x season sheet, then two scenes."""
import numpy as np, os, sys
from wpaint import *
from species import paint_species
from context import gravel, grass, water, scene_palette, cast_shadow, WATERB
from stage4 import sky_ground
from sheets import pad_to, label_grid
OUT = "stage7"; os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
L0 = sun(25, 40)


def cell(species, season, W_, H_, seed=1, cw=170, ch=150, p=None, ground="gravel"):
    cv = sky_ground(cw, ch, ch - 22); gy = ch - 12
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    gm = np.zeros((ch, cw), bool); gm[ch - 22:] = True
    if ground == "grass":
        grass(cv, gm, rng, L0, blade=(1, 2), dens=0.4, crest=False)
    else:
        gravel(cv, gm, rng, L0, y_near=ch, y_far=ch - 22, stone=(0.8, 2.0), dens=0.3)
    cast_shadow(cv, cw / 2, gy, W_, H_, L0, fore=0.15, rng=rng)
    paint_species(cv, species, cw / 2, gy, W_, H_, L0, rng, p=p, foot=ground)
    return cv.render(scene_palette(season, "noon"))


ROWS = [
    ("willow", 110, 80, dict(), ["summer", "silvery", "autumn"], None),
    ("winter_willow", 110, 80, dict(), ["winter_red", "winter_yellow", "winter_grey"], None),
    ("sitka_alder", 120, 70, dict(), ["alder", "summer", "autumn"], None),
    ("dwarf_willow", 130, 14, dict(catkin_p=0.12), ["alpine", "summer", "autumn"], "grass"),
    ("red_alder", 70, 125, dict(), ["alder", "autumn", "winter_grey"], None),
]

if __name__ == "__main__":
    cells, rowlab = [], []
    for sp, W_, H_, p, seasons, gr in ROWS:
        for i, s in enumerate(seasons):

            if sp == "red_alder" and s.startswith("winter"):

    Hc = max(c.shape[0] for c in cells); Wc = max(c.shape[1] for c in cells)
    cells = [pad_to(c, Wc, Hc) for c in cells]
    save(label_grid(cells, 3, rowlabels=rowlab, collabels=["season 1", "season 2", "season 3"],
                    title="stage 7: species on one pipeline (columns: willow summer/silvery/autumn; winter red/yellow/grey; alder: summer/summer-willow palette/autumn; dwarf: alpine/summer/autumn; red alder: summer/autumn/winter)"),

def riparian(season="summer", seed=21, w=360, h=200):
    """A west-side gravel-bed river: a band of red alder behind (pale trunks), willow scrub on the
    bar in front of it, the channel with its reflection, the near gravel. Winter: the same
    geometry with bare alders (mauve-grey haze over white trunks) and leafless willow."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    winter = season.startswith("winter")
    cv = sky_ground(w, h, 70)
    bank = np.zeros((h, w), bool); bank[70:112] = True
    gravel(cv, bank, rng, L0, y_near=112, y_far=70, stone=(0.6, 1.6), dens=0.3)
    x = -10
    while x < w + 10:                                    # alder band
        W_ = rng.uniform(22, 40); H_ = W_ * rng.uniform(1.7, 2.3)
        paint_species(cv, "red_alder", x, int(92 + rng.uniform(-2, 2)), W_, H_, L0, rng, band=1, p=dict(bare=winter))
        x += W_ * rng.uniform(0.45, 0.8)
    x = -10
    while x < w + 10:                                    # willow scrub on the bar
        W_ = rng.uniform(26, 60)
        sp = "winter_willow" if winter else "willow"
        paint_species(cv, sp, x, int(110 + rng.uniform(-2, 2)), W_, W_ * rng.uniform(0.55, 0.8), L0, rng, band=0, foot="gravel")
        x += W_ * rng.uniform(0.5, 1.1)
    wm = np.zeros((h, w), bool); wm[112:160] = True
    water(cv, wm, 112, rng, dash_p=0.06)
    near = np.zeros((h, w), bool); near[160:] = True
    gravel(cv, near, rng, L0, y_near=h, y_far=160, stone=(1.5, 5.0), dens=0.5)
    return cv


def run_scenes():
    for s, pal in (("summer", "summer"), ("autumn", "autumn"), ("winter", "winter_grey")):
        cv = riparian("winter" if s == "winter" else s)
        img = cv.render(scene_palette(pal, "golden" if s == "autumn" else "noon"))
        save(img, f"{OUT}/s7_riparian_{s}_1x.png"); save(img, f"{OUT}/s7_riparian_{s}_3x.png", 3)
E
nice -n 10 python3 -c "import stage7; stage7.run_scenes()" 2>&1 | grep -v Warn | tail -3

# output:
return v / np.linalg.norm(v, axis=-1, keepdims=True)

Bash: Recursive bare crown for alders
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow && python3 - <<'E'
s=open('species.py').read()
s=s.replace("# ------------------------------------------------------------------ broadleaf glyph (alders)",'''def paint_bare_crown(cv, x0, y0, W_, H_, L, rng, depth=4, mat=STEM):
    """A leafless tree crown: branches fork recursively from the trunk top (clean runs), each
    generation shorter and more spread; the outermost generation is a fine twig haze drawn as
    single pixels at the crown's edge (a two-step dither between twig and sky, Ferrari's way of
    saying 'too fine to draw'). The crown's outline comes out an oval because branch lengths are
    set by an ellipse around the crown centre."""
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    lx = -np.sign(L[0]) if abs(L[0]) > 0.05 else 1
    ccx, ccy = x0, y0 - H_ * 0.5
    ra, rb = W_ / 2, H_ / 2
    def inside(x, y):
        return ((x - ccx) / ra) ** 2 + ((y - ccy) / rb) ** 2 < 1
    def put(x, y, s):
        if 0 <= x < w and 0 <= y < h:
            if cv.mat[y, x] == mat and cv.step[y, x] >= s:
                return
            cv.mat[y, x] = mat; cv.step[y, x] = s
    def branch(x, y, ang, length, gen):
        ex, ey = x + np.cos(ang) * length, y + np.sin(ang) * length
        # stop at the oval
        k = 1.0
        while not inside(ex, ey) and k > 0.3:
            k -= 0.1; ex, ey = x + np.cos(ang) * length * k, y + np.sin(ang) * length * k
        s = 1 if gen == 0 else (3 if np.cos(ang) * lx < -0.2 and gen >= 2 else 2)
        for (px, py) in clean_line(x, y, ex, ey):
            put(px, py, s)
        if gen >= depth:
            # twig haze: a spray of single pixels beyond the tip, inside the oval
            for _ in range(int(3 + length)):
                a = ang + rng.normal(0, 0.8); d = rng.uniform(1, length * 0.9 + 2)
                tx, ty = int(round(ex + np.cos(a) * d)), int(round(ey + np.sin(a) * d))
                if inside(tx, ty) and rng.random() < 0.7:
                    put(tx, ty, 2 if rng.random() < 0.6 else 3)
            return
        nb = int(rng.integers(2, 4))
        for i in range(nb):
            a = ang + rng.uniform(-0.75, 0.75) - 0.15 * np.sin(ang)   # a slight upward bias
            branch(ex, ey, a, length * rng.uniform(0.55, 0.75), gen + 1)
    for i in range(int(rng.integers(3, 6))):
        a = -np.pi / 2 + rng.uniform(-1.1, 1.1)
        branch(x0, y0 - H_ * 0.12, a, H_ * rng.uniform(0.28, 0.4), 1)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ broadleaf glyph (alders)''')
s=s.replace("""        paint_winter(tmp, cx, int(crown_bot - Hc * 0.1), W_, Hc * 0.95, L, rng,""","""        paint_bare_crown(tmp, cx, int(crown_bot + Hc * 0.05), W_ * 0.95, Hc, L, rng)
    elif False:
        paint_winter(tmp, cx, int(crown_bot - Hc * 0.1), W_, Hc * 0.95, L, rng,""")
open('species.py','w').write(s)
E

s=open('LESSONS.md').read()
s=s.replace("- **What would help**: a reference set of willow on braided-river gravel photographed from 20–40° above\n  (for the oblique camera), and a back-lit willow set. More species would interest me later: dwarf\n  willow mats and alder scrub would reuse the whole pipeline with a new mark and a new wand model.\n","")
s+='''

---

## Round two: the open items

New references in `refs/oblique/` (Openverse: willow on gravel, back-lit willows, willow from above).
The two that taught the most: a back-lit willow crown (`openverse_week_45_Sunshine_*`) and willow
scrub around a tent on a gravel flat (`openverse_2007_08_03_*`).

- **Back light is an inversion, not a rim.** In the photo the whole crown glows pale yellow-green
  wherever it is thin, and the twigs are dark lines across the glow. I had only a 3 px rim, so a
  back-lit bush was a dark blob. Now (`bush.py`, "transmitted light") the lit family is set by an
  estimated thickness: strongest at the silhouette and in the upper crown (a decay length of a
  quarter of the bush width), fading into the thick core and the base; and with the sun behind, the
  wands through the canopy are drawn in the darkest twig step, 1.6× as many, over the leaves.
  Still to do: the transmitted light should be a warmer, yellower ramp than reflected light.
- **The oblique haystack had three causes.** (1) A sign error: seeing the bush from 30° above
  tilts the world's up toward the viewer, so the light must rotate by +30°; I had −30°, which put the
  sun behind and made every oblique bush dark at noon. (2) The skirt curtain plus the base arc made a
  triangle; the oblique camera now has no curtain and a flatter crown (`top_flat`). (3) The real fix:
  from above, a wand's depth moves it down the screen (`view_tilt`: y' = y·cos30 + z·sin30), so the
  crown spreads into sub-crowns at different screen heights instead of stacking into one cone
  (sheet E).
- **The shadow side's edge**: more holes and caps on the shadow-family silhouette, and dark wand
  ticks there at 1.4× the lit side's rate.
- **The repeating treeline**: each row now starts with a low understorey of small willows (0.35–0.7
  of the row width, flattened), so the row's base is continuous; the crowns above take lognormal
  sizes (0.4–2.2×), heights 0.5–0.95 and occasional openings. The reflection's lights drop more and
  lose 20% chroma toward the water colour (critic round 8: "Rorschach blot").
- **Airy holes** open only near the original silhouette (they had punched floating sky specks into
  the middle of big bushes once the clumps left gaps).

---

## Stage 7: more species on the same pipeline

![species](stage7/s7_species_x_season.png)

`species.py`. Each species is a change of form (the wand model), mark and palette; the clump grammar,
value plan, fringe, level of detail, contact, cast shadow and depth bands are all shared.

- **Winter willow** (leafless): only wands and twigs, 1-px clean runs, red, yellow or mauve-grey. It
  reads only when the stems are layered by depth: back wands one step down (seen through the front
  ones), front sun-facing wands lit, the rest mid. With one value it was a solid colour blob.
- **Sitka alder**: the willow form made sprawling (splay 85°, stems diverging early, flat top) with
  the broadleaf mark, which is the C1 lozenge leaf from the master copy (`broadleaf_marks`). The
  Ferrari copy was of a broadleaf bush, so the alders are closer to his vocabulary than the willow.
- **Dwarf willow**: a mat 10–15% as tall as wide, clumps of 1.5–3 px, leaves as dots and pairs,
  catkins as 1–2 px silver uprights on the crest. Not done: draping over rock (it sits on flat
  ground as a low hedge).
- **Red alder**: 1–3 pale trunks (lit side, body, shadow side, white lichen pixels, dark lenticel
  dashes) converging into the crown. The crown is the willow form with branches leaving the trunk at
  different heights (`base_rise`), foliage from 30% along each branch, and not clipped at its own
  base (it clipped flat, like a cloud on sticks). Autumn alder is green-brown, not gold (alders drop
  their leaves green), its own palette.
- **Bare alder crowns needed their own painter** (`paint_bare_crown`). The wand fan made brooms and
  feather dusters, however I set it. A recursive fork does it: 3–5 main branches, each forking 2–3
  ways, shorter and more spread each time, clipped to an oval; the last generation is a haze of single
  twig pixels. Branches facing the sun at the outer generations get the lit step.

![riparian summer](stage7/s7_riparian_summer_3x.png)
![riparian autumn](stage7/s7_riparian_autumn_3x.png)
![riparian winter](stage7/s7_riparian_winter_3x.png)

A west-side gravel-bed river (`stage7.riparian`), after guide 04's "100 m" description: an alder
stand with white trunks behind, willow scrub on the bar, the channel with its reflection, near gravel.
The same geometry in summer, autumn and winter (bare mauve alders over white trunks, leafless willow).

**Honest state of the species.** The winter stand and the summer riparian scene read at 1×. The
single red alder is still a lollipop at 3× (its crown is too regular), the dwarf willow is a hedge,
not a mat, and Sitka alder is a broadleaf willow rather than its own sprawling J-stemmed habit.
Candidates next, all from guide 04 and all cheap on this pipeline: red-osier dogwood (red winter
stems: the winter willow with a new ramp), salmonberry thickets, black cottonwood (the bare-crown
painter plus a furrowed trunk), and reed canarygrass swards.
'''
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)
E
echo ok

SubagentHandback:

Round two is done: I fetched the references I'd asked for, fixed all four open items, and added four species with three river scenes. Everything is in ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/willow/, and LESSONS.md has two new sections ("Round two: the open items" and "Stage 7"). All outputs were re-rendered after the fixes. The critic hasn't reviewed any of this round yet.

New references. 33 photos in refs/oblique/ (willow on gravel, back-lit willows, willow from above), plus species photos in refs/species/. Two changed the method: a back-lit willow crown, and willow scrub on a gravel flat.

The four open items:

  • Back-lit glow. The photo showed this is an inversion, not a rim. The whole crown glows wherever it is thin, and the twigs are dark lines across it. The back-lit cells in sheets/D_camera_x_light.png now glow.
  • Oblique "haystack". There were three causes:
  • A sign error in the camera tilt had put the sun behind every oblique bush, so they were dark at noon.
  • The skirt plus the curved base made a triangle.
  • The real fix: seen from above, a stem's depth now moves it down the screen. The crown spreads into several sub-crowns instead of one cone (sheets/E_season_x_size_oblique.png).
  • Shadow-side edge. That edge now has more holes and small bumps, and dark twig ticks at 1.4 times the rate of the lit side.
  • Repeating treeline. Each row now starts with a low understorey, so its base is continuous. The crowns above vary widely in size and height, with occasional gaps. Reflections are also toned down further.

New species (species.py, sheet stage7/s7_species_x_season.png):

  • Winter willow: leafless red, yellow or mauve-grey stems. It only reads when back stems are darker than front, sun-facing ones.
  • Sitka alder: the willow shape made sprawling, using the broadleaf mark from my Ferrari copy.
  • Dwarf willow: a low mat with dot leaves and silver catkins.
  • Red alder: pale, lichen-patched trunks converging into a crown; its autumn is green-brown, not gold. Bare winter crowns needed their own painter (branches forking into a haze of fine twigs), because the stem fan kept producing brooms.

River scenes. stage7/s7_riparian_{summer,autumn,winter}_3x.png follow the riparian guide (04): an alder stand with white trunks, willow scrub on the bar, the channel with its reflection, and gravel in front. The summer and winter versions read well at 1×.

Not there yet:

  • A single red alder still looks like a lollipop at 3×.
  • The dwarf willow is a low hedge rather than a mat draped over rock.
  • Sitka alder lacks its own curved, J-shaped stems.
  • Back-lit leaves should be a warmer, yellower colour than sunlit ones.
  • The gravel still lies in rows; I haven't tried the channel and cobbles students' gravel yet.

Candidates next: red-osier dogwood (the winter willow with a new colour), salmonberry thickets, black cottonwood (the bare-crown painter plus a furrowed trunk) and reed canarygrass. All of these are quick to add on this pipeline.

# Ready to use: the second studio (2026-09-26 night)

These are the techniques the second studio critic judged Ferrari-grade or ready to use. The critic ran 10 rounds on eight students, looking at 4–10× crops beside the students' photos and Ferrari's own pixels. Each item points to the student's folder, where the code, the lessons and the closing read in CRITIQUE.md live. Offered to the voxsim painting lanes as a resource, not a spec. None of the eight sets is at Ferrari's bar at 8× across the board; every student's "Where it stands" says so, and I agree with each of them.

**Masses first, for ground** (elements/ground: LESSONS "The rules to take even without the code"; sheets B and F; drop-in `ground.paint_ground(..., shadow_mask=)`). This is the studio's first answer to READY.md's main open problem.

- Decide two to four designed lit and shadow masses first, from off-frame casters and the landform.
- Keep shadow near black with 2–3 values, and put the colour in the light.
- Marks go inside a mass and never cross the terminator, except as a fringe of tips.
- Use ordered 2×2 dither for mid and far turns. Use runs only near.
- Best images: `sheets/F_scenes.png` (meadow bank, golden) and `sheets/B_landform_x_sun.png` (hummocks and contre-jour).

**Zoom handoff without pops** (elements/grass-lod: LESSONS §1–2, 6). Each tuft gets one stroke-size number, and every discrete choice is dithered against the tuft's own random numbers. Checked frame by frame across 100× zoom at four hours. The pan test and pop metric are reusable for any material.

**Conifers** (elements/conifers; best: `sheets/E_stand_x_hour.png`, `F_zoom_strips_1x.png`)

- Four PNW species at four ages with naturalist-grade form: nodding hemlock, cedar J-limbs with fluted buttress and candelabra, spruce curtains, the old Douglas-fir pillar.
- **Tier-top light** from V16: light on the upper surface of every tier on both sides of the stem, not on the sun-side half.
- Side-view stand relit at four hours by palette alone.

**Broadleaves** (elements/broadleaf; best: `stage5/contact_v7.png`, sheet A)

- Pacific madrone and Garry oak crowns. The oak comes from per-species elbow kinks and open crowns with limbs showing in the holes.
- Bark and moss: the checker-banded cylinder read from V30 ("pure / 50% / pure, never non-adjacent steps"), a moss sheath with drips and a seam, madrone peel.
- An LOD rule with no vanishing: a clump always emits a spray, and a spray always emits a mark.
- Phenology specific to each species.

**Sagebrush steppe** (elements/steppe; best: `hero/sage_flat_golden_3x.png`, `stage5/seasons_final`)

- The eye-level sage flat at golden hour, and the steppe's four seasons.
- The sage form: low, wider than tall, with vertical sprig streaks and a dark base.
- The bunchgrass tussock, and ponderosa bark.
- A method to lend everyone: box-downsample the photo to game resolution and paint from that.

    """A willow: a fan of wands from a root crown, each arching out and drooping at the tip.
    Leaf clusters (lobes) sit along the outer part of each wand, so the silhouette is the sum of
    wand tips, and stems show where clusters are sparse (low down, between wands)."""

    def __init__(self, cx, gy, W, H, rng, nw=None, spread=75, droop=0.45, leafy_from=0.12,
                 lobe_r=(0.055, 0.11), lobe_gap=0.9, lift=0.0, lean=0.0, crown_w=0.12, arch_w=0.32, skirt_n=3, nclumps=None, clump_sd=(9.0, 0.35), clump_mix=0.35, cap_t=0.55, cap_reach=0.35, diverge=0.32, rim=1.4, rim_w=3.5, glow=1.0, curtain=1, egg_c=0.55, top_flat=0.0, view_tilt=0.0, clip_ground=True, base_rise=0.0):

        self.base_rise = base_rise

        self.curtain = curtain

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ trees: skeleton, bark, crown
def paint_trunk(cv, segs, L, rng, style="cottonwood", mat=BARK):
    """Bark on a limb path. segs: list of (x0, y0, w0, x1, y1, w1). Across the limb: lit side,
    body, shadow side, with the handover between steps as a 2x2 checker band (V30: pure, 50%,
    pure; never skip a step). Cottonwood: deep vertical furrows (dark runs 3-9 px) with a lit ridge
    beside each on the sun side. Alder: white lichen blocks and dark lenticel dashes."""
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    lx = -np.sign(L[0]) if abs(L[0]) > 0.05 else 1
    furrows = {}
    for (x0, y0, w0, x1, y1, w1) in segs:
        n = int(max(abs(x1 - x0), abs(y1 - y0))) + 1
        for i in range(n):
            t = i / max(n - 1, 1)
            xc, yc, hw = x0 + (x1 - x0) * t, y0 + (y1 - y0) * t, max(0.5, (w0 + (w1 - w0) * t) / 2)
            y = int(round(yc))
            for x in range(int(np.floor(xc - hw)), int(np.ceil(xc + hw)) + 1):
                if not (0 <= x < w and 0 <= y < h):
                    continue
                u = (x - xc) / max(hw, 0.5)
                if abs(u) > 1.02:
                    continue
                v = -u * lx                        # +1 toward the sun
                # steps: 4 lit | 3 body | 2 turning | 1 shadow; 50% checker at each handover
                c = (x + y) % 2
                if v > 0.45:
                    s = 4
                elif v > 0.25:
                    s = 4 if c else 3
                elif v > -0.2:
                    s = 3
                elif v > -0.4:
                    s = 3 if c else 2
                elif v > -0.65:
                    s = 2 if c else 1
                else:
                    s = 1
                if style == "cottonwood" and hw >= 1.5:
                    # furrows: fixed columns across the trunk (in limb coordinates) that break and resume
                    col = int(round((u + 1) * hw))
                    key = (round(xc / 3), col)
                    if col % 3 == 1 and rng.random() > 0.08:
                        s = 0 if v < 0.3 else 1
                    elif col % 3 == 2 and v > 0.2:
                        s = min(5, s + 1)       # the ridge beside a furrow catches the light
                elif style == "alder":
                    if rng.random() < 0.12:
                        s = 5
                    elif hw >= 1.5 and rng.random() < 0.04:
                        s = 0
                cv.mat[y, x] = mat; cv.step[y, x] = s


class TreeCrown(WillowBush):
    """A tree for the foliage pipeline: a recursive limb skeleton in 3-D; every limb is a 'wand'
    (so wand_dirs and the visible-wand pass work unchanged), and leaf lobes sit on the outer
    generations, clumped per main limb (so each main limb makes one sub-crown)."""

    def __init__(self, cx, gy, W, H, rng, fork=0.4, limbs=(3, 6), depth=3, up=(15, 45), spread=0.8,
                 lobe_r=(0.07, 0.12), gap=0.8, ragged=0.35, **kw):
        WillowBush.__init__(self, cx, gy, W, H, rng, nw=1, **kw)
        self.wands, self.lobes, self.segs = [], [], []
        top = np.array([cx, gy - H * fork, 0.0])
        self.trunk = (cx, gy, top)
        nl = int(rng.integers(limbs[0], limbs[1] + 1))
        self.nclumps = nl
        for li in range(nl):
            az = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
            tilt = np.radians(rng.uniform(*up))
            L0 = H * (1 - fork) * rng.uniform(0.55, 0.8)
            self._grow(top, tilt, az, L0, W * 0.07, 1, depth, li, rng, spread, lobe_r, gap, ragged)

    def _grow(self, p0, tilt, az, length, width, gen, depth, li, rng, spread, lobe_r, gap, ragged):
        W, H = self.W, self.H
        d = np.array([np.sin(tilt) * np.cos(az) * W / H * 1.2, -np.cos(tilt), np.sin(tilt) * np.sin(az) * W / H * 1.2])
        p1 = p0 + d * length
        # resample as a wand (15 points, slight upward curve)
        pts = np.array([p0 + (p1 - p0) * t + np.array([0, -0.08 * length * np.sin(np.pi * t), 0]) for t in np.linspace(0, 1, 15)])
        wi = len(self.wands)
        self.wands.append(dict(pts=pts, az=az, tilt=tilt))
        self.segs.append((p0[0], p0[1], width, p1[0], p1[1], width * 0.65, p0[2]))
        if gen >= 2:
            t = 0.35 + rng.uniform(0, 0.15)
            while t <= 1.0:
                k = t * 14; j = min(int(k), 13); f = k - j
                p = pts[j] * (1 - f) + pts[j + 1] * f
                r = W * rng.uniform(*lobe_r) * (0.8 + 0.5 * t)
                self.lobes.append(dict(x=p[0], y=p[1], r=r, z=p[2] + W * 0.35, u=0, v=0, wand=wi, t=t, clump=li))
                t += gap * r / max(length, 1) + 0.05
        if gen >= depth or rng.random() < ragged * (gen - 1) * 0.5:
            if gen >= 2:
                return
        nb = int(rng.integers(2, 4))
        for i in range(nb):
            tp = rng.uniform(0.45, 1.0)
            q = p0 + (p1 - p0) * tp
            self._grow(q, np.clip(tilt + rng.uniform(-spread, spread) * 0.7, 0.0, 1.9), az + rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0),
                       length * rng.uniform(0.45, 0.65), width * 0.6, gen + 1, depth, li, rng, spread, lobe_r, gap, ragged)


def paint_cottonwood(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Black cottonwood: a tall furrowed grey trunk, a few big upswept limbs, an open ragged crown
    of separate billowing clumps with sky between; leaves dark glossy above and silver-white below
    (they flutter: many silver flecks); autumn yellow; winter a bare upswept crown (p['bare'])."""
    p = p or {}
    h, w = cv.h, cv.w
    tc = TreeCrown(cx, gy, W_, H_, rng, fork=p.get("fork", rng.uniform(0.35, 0.5)), limbs=(3, 5), depth=3, up=(10, 40),
                   lobe_r=(0.07, 0.11), gap=0.7, ragged=0.4, clip_ground=False, curtain=0, clump_mix=0.6, egg_c=0.45)
    top = tc.trunk[2]
    tw = max(2.0, W_ * 0.09)
    segs = [(cx, gy, tw * 1.3, top[0], top[1], tw)] + [(s[0], s[1], max(1.0, s[2] * (1.6 if i < tc.nclumps else 1)), s[3], s[4],
                                                        max(1.0, s[5]), ) for i, s in enumerate(tc.segs)]
    if p.get("bare"):
        paint_trunk(cv, segs[:1], L, rng, "cottonwood")
        for s in segs[1:]:
            for (px, py) in clean_line(s[0], s[1], s[3], s[4]):
                for k in range(int(max(1, round(s[2] * 0.6)))):
                    if 0 <= px + k < w and 0 <= py < h:
                        cv.mat[py, px + k] = STEM; cv.step[py, px + k] = 2 if k == 0 else 1
        for wd in tc.wands:
            p1 = wd["pts"][-1]
            paint_bare_crown(cv, p1[0], p1[1] + H_ * 0.05, W_ * 0.3, H_ * 0.22, L, rng, depth=3)
        return None
    q = merge(lod_params(W_), dict(bush_obj=tc, no_contact=True, broadleaf=dict(lens=(2.0, 3.5)),
                                   clump=dict(r=(max(2.0, W_ * 0.045), max(3.5, W_ * 0.08)), aspect=1.1),
                                   sprays=None, stems=dict(p=0.0, see=0.0), front_sprays=None,
                                   wands_visible=dict(p=0.8, t=(0.0, 0.8), brk=0.08),
                                   airy=dict(below=0.0, depth=12, scale=3.0, amount=0.45, cl=0.45),
                                   tips=dict(p=0.1, lens=(1, 2))))
    tmp = Canvas(w, h)
    r = paint_willow(tmp, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, merge(q, p.get("crown", {})))
    # silver flecks: cottonwood leaves turn their pale undersides in any wind
    sil = (tmp.mat == LEAF) & (tmp.step >= 3) & (rng.random((h, w)) < p.get("silver", 0.22))
    tmp.mat[sil] = SILVER
    # trunk and limbs behind the crown, crown over them
    paint_trunk(cv, segs, L, rng, "cottonwood")
    m = tmp.mat != 0
    cv.mat[m] = tmp.mat[m]; cv.step[m] = tmp.step[m]
    return r


def paint_dogwood(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Red-osier dogwood. Leafless: a thicket of straight upright red stems (fewer, stiffer and more
    upright than willow), paired twigs at the nodes. In leaf: opposite oval leaves (the broadleaf
    mark), flat white flower heads in June, white berries in autumn (p['flowers'] / p['berries'])."""
    p = p or {}
    if p.get("bare"):
        return paint_winter(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, dict(form=dict(nw=int(16 + W_ * 0.3), spread=30, droop=0.02,
                                                                          diverge=0.08, crown_w=0.55), twig_p=0.25))
    q = merge(lod_params(W_), dict(form=dict(spread=45, droop=0.25, diverge=0.15, crown_w=0.5, lobe_r=(0.07, 0.12),
                                             nw=int(14 + W_ * 0.15)),
                                   clump=dict(r=(max(2.0, W_ * 0.045), max(3.5, W_ * 0.08)), aspect=1.2),
                                   sprays=None, broadleaf=dict(lens=(2.0, 3.5)), stems=dict(p=0.6, see=1.2),
                                   front_sprays=None, tips=dict(p=0.15, lens=(1, 3))))
    r = paint_willow(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, q)
    dots = p.get("flowers", 0) or p.get("berries", 0)
    if dots:
        M = r["M"]
        cand = np.argwhere(M & (np.roll(M, 1, 0) == False) | (M & (rng.random(M.shape) < 0.01)))
        for (y, x) in cand:
            if rng.random() > dots:
                continue
            # a flat-topped cyme: 3 px wide, 1-2 tall, white; berries a looser 2-px cluster
            shape = [(-1, 0), (0, 0), (1, 0), (0, -1)] if p.get("flowers") else [(0, 0), (1, 1), (-1, 1)]
            for dx, dy in shape:
                yy, xx = y + dy, x + dx
                if 0 <= yy < cv.h and 0 <= xx < cv.w:
                    cv.mat[yy, xx] = SILVER; cv.step[yy, xx] = 4 if dy <= 0 else 3
    return r


def paint_salmonberry(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, p=None):
    """Salmonberry thicket: arching canes 1-4 m that bow over at the top (strong droop), broad
    three-part leaves in large flat clumps, magenta flowers in spring, orange-red berries in early
    summer (p['flowers'] / p['berries']), peeling orange-brown canes in winter (p['bare'])."""
    p = p or {}
    if p.get("bare"):
        return paint_winter(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, dict(form=dict(nw=int(12 + W_ * 0.2), spread=55, droop=0.7,
                                                                          diverge=0.1, crown_w=0.7), twig_p=0.2))
    q = merge(lod_params(W_), dict(form=dict(spread=60, droop=0.75, diverge=0.12, crown_w=0.7, lobe_r=(0.08, 0.14),
                                             nw=int(12 + W_ * 0.14), lobe_gap=0.7, top_flat=0.3),
                                   clump=dict(r=(max(2.0, W_ * 0.05), max(4.0, W_ * 0.1)), aspect=1.5),
                                   sprays=None, broadleaf=dict(lens=(2.5, 4.5), wmax=(1.1, 1.8), angles=(0, 26.6)),
                                   stems=dict(p=0.5, see=1.0), front_sprays=None, tips=dict(p=0.1, lens=(1, 3))))
    r = paint_willow(cv, cx, gy, W_, H_, L, rng, q)
    dots = p.get("flowers", 0) or p.get("berries", 0)
    if dots:
        M = r["M"]
        for (y, x) in np.argwhere(M):
            if rng.random() > dots * 0.04:
                continue
            m = 16 if p.get("flowers") else 17      # magenta flower / orange berry ramps
            for dx, dy in ([(0, 0), (1, 0)] if p.get("flowers") else [(0, 0)]):
                if 0 <= x + dx < cv.w:
                    cv.mat[y + dy, x + dx] = m; cv.step[y + dy, x + dx] = 1 if dx == 0 else 0
    return r


SPECIES.update(black_cottonwood=paint_cottonwood, dogwood=paint_dogwood, salmonberry=paint_salmonberry)

s=open('LESSONS.md').read()
s+='''

---

## Stage 7, continued: dogwood, salmonberry, black cottonwood, and mixed stands

- **Red-osier dogwood** (`paint_dogwood`): leafless, a thicket of straight upright red stems, stiffer,
  fewer and more upright than willow (splay 30°, almost no droop, few twigs). In leaf it is the
  broadleaf mark with flat white flower heads (3-px cymes on the crest) in June and loose white berry
  clusters in autumn, when the leaves go purple-red (`dogwood_autumn`).
- **Salmonberry** (`paint_salmonberry`): arching canes that bow over at the top (droop 0.75), big
  flat three-part leaves (wide lozenges near horizontal), magenta flowers in spring, orange berries in
  early summer, peeling orange-brown canes in winter.
- **Black cottonwood** (`paint_cottonwood`, `TreeCrown`): the first real tree on this pipeline. A
  recursive 3-D limb skeleton is handed to the willow painter as if each limb were a wand
  (`p["bush_obj"]`), so the clump grammar, marks, fringe, airy holes and visible-wand pass all work
  unchanged; lobes sit on the outer limb generations, one sub-crown per main limb. Leaves flutter:
  22% of the lit leaves are silver. Autumn yellow; winter an upswept bare crown (a twig haze around
  each outer limb). Bark (`paint_trunk`) is its own dark ramp with vertical furrows and a lit ridge
  beside each on the sun side; the handovers across the trunk are 2×2 checker bands, never skipping a
  step (the V30 rule the broadleaf student read). Two bugs worth recording: limbs drawn after the
  trunk overwrote it with their own lit side, so each junction lit up as a white band (now the first
  painter of a pixel keeps it); and the trunk rasteriser skipped rows when a leaning segment rounded
  two samples onto one y (now oversampled).
- **Each species wears its own palette in a mixed scene.** Materials are now 16-bit; each species
  group gets a material offset (210, a multiple of the band offset so band arithmetic still works)
  and its own seasonal palette (`context.GROUP_SEASON`). In autumn that gives the naturalist's
  stepped profile: yellow cottonwood crowns standing ragged above green-brown alders, gold willow on
  the bar, purple-red dogwood at the bank toe. Before, one palette per scene turned the alders gold,
  which is wrong: alders drop their leaves green.

![species](stage7/s7_species_x_season.png)
![riparian autumn](stage7/s7_riparian_autumn_3x.png)

**Honest state.** The mixed autumn riparian scene is the best of the new work. Single trees are the
weak spot: the cottonwood reads as a Y-forked generic tree at 2× (its crown should be more ragged and
taller, with hanging lower limbs), and the single red alder is still regular. The bare-crown painter
is good in stands and thin on one isolated tree. Dogwood and salmonberry in leaf are too alike;
their difference is mostly their flowers and fruit.
'''
open('LESSONS.md','w').write(s)

164. elements/willow/stage7/s7_riparian_winter_3x.png

SubagentHandback:

Red-osier dogwood, salmonberry and black cottonwood are now painted on the willow pipeline. The river scenes use them in summer, autumn and winter. I looked at every render myself; the critic hasn't reviewed this round yet. The write-up is under "Stage 7, continued" in LESSONS.md.

The three species (species.py):

  • Red-osier dogwood: in winter, a thicket of straight, stiff, upright red stems. In leaf it has broad leaves, small flat white flower heads in June, white berries in autumn and purple-red autumn leaves.
  • Salmonberry: canes that arch over at the top, big flat three-part leaves, magenta flowers in spring, orange berries in early summer, and peeling orange-brown canes in winter.
  • Black cottonwood: the first real tree on this pipeline.
  • Each branch of a 3-D skeleton is painted as if it were a willow stem, so all the foliage code works unchanged, with one sub-crown per main limb.
  • About a fifth of the lit leaves show silver, as cottonwood leaves do in wind. Autumn is yellow, and winter is a bare, upswept crown.
  • The bark is dark grey with vertical furrows and a lit ridge on the sun side of each one.
  • I fixed two trunk bugs: limb junctions showed as white bands across the trunk, and leaning trunks skipped pixel rows.

Each species keeps its own colours in a mixed scene. Before, one palette per scene turned the alders gold in autumn, which is wrong: alders drop their leaves green-brown. The autumn scene (stage7/s7_riparian_autumn_3x.png) is the best of the new work. It shows the stepped profile the naturalist guide describes: yellow cottonwood crowns standing ragged above green-brown alders, gold willow on the gravel bar, and purple-red dogwood at the bank. The winter scene has mauve bare crowns over white alder trunks, with red dogwood and orange salmonberry stems along the bank. The summer scene and the full species × season sheet are in stage7/.

Not good enough yet:

  • A single cottonwood reads as a generic Y-forked tree; its crown should be more ragged and taller, with hanging lower limbs.
  • A single red alder is still too regular. Bare crowns look right in stands but thin on an isolated tree.
  • Dogwood and salmonberry in leaf look alike; only their flowers and fruit tell them apart.

def __init__(self, cx, gy, W, H, rng, fork=0.4, limbs=(3, 6), depth=3, up=(15, 45), spread=0.8,
                 lobe_r=(0.07, 0.12), gap=0.8, ragged=0.35, **kw):
        WillowBush.__init__(self, cx, gy, W, H, rng, nw=1, **kw)
        self.wands, self.lobes, self.segs = [], [], []

        self.nclumps = nl

    def _grow(self, p0, tilt, az, length, width, gen, depth, li, rng, spread, lobe_r, gap, ragged):
        W, H = self.W, self.H
        d = np.array([np.sin(tilt) * np.cos(az) * W / H * 1.2, -np.cos(tilt), np.sin(tilt) * np.sin(az) * W / H * 1.2])
        p1 = p0 + d * length

        self.wands, self.lobes, self.segs = [], [], []
        th = H * fork
        top = np.array([cx + lean * th, gy - th, 0.0])
        self.trunk = (cx, gy, top)
        nl = int(rng.integers(limbs[0], limbs[1] + 1))
        self.nclumps = nl + (1 if leader else 0)
        for li in range(nl):
            az = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
            u = rng.uniform(0, 1) if low > 0 else 1.0
            f = 1 - low * (1 - u)                       # where on the trunk the limb leaves (1 = top)
            base = np.array([cx + lean * th * f, gy - th * f, 0.0])
            if rng.random() < hang_p:
                tilt = np.radians(rng.uniform(70, 105))
                L0 = H * (1 - fork) * rng.uniform(0.35, 0.55)
            else:
                tilt = np.radians(rng.uniform(*up))
                L0 = H * (1 - fork) * rng.uniform(0.6, 0.85) * (0.8 + 0.2 * f)
            self._grow(base, tilt, az, L0, W * 0.07, 1, depth, li, rng, spread, lobe_r, gap, ragged)
        if leader:
            self._grow(top, np.radians(rng.uniform(0, 8)), rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi), H * (1 - fork) * 0.95,
                       W * 0.06, 1, depth, nl, rng, spread * 0.8, lobe_r, gap, ragged)""")
s=s.replace("""    tc = TreeCrown(cx, gy, W_, H_, rng, fork=p.get("fork", rng.uniform(0.28, 0.4)), limbs=(3, 5), depth=3, up=(18, 55),
                   lobe_r=(0.08, 0.14), gap=0.65, ragged=0.4, clip_ground=False, curtain=0, clump_mix=0.6, egg_c=0.45)""","""    # cottonwood: a central leader through a tall open crown; limbs leave over the upper third of the
    # trunk; some leave flat and droop, so the lower crown is ragged; billows kept apart by sky
    tc = TreeCrown(cx, gy, W_, H_, rng, fork=p.get("fork", rng.uniform(0.25, 0.34)), limbs=(4, 7), depth=3, up=(15, 55),
                   lobe_r=(0.07, 0.12), gap=1.0, ragged=0.5, clip_ground=False, curtain=0, clump_mix=0.65, egg_c=0.45,
